Non-Additive Geometry and Frobenius Correspondences
Shai Haran

Abstract
The usual language of algebraic geometry is not appropriate for Arithmetical geometry: addition is singular at the real prime. We developed two languages that overcome this problem: one replace rings by the collection of “vectors” or by bi-operads and another based on “matrices” or props. These are the two languages of [ Har17 ] , but we omit the involutions which brings considerable simplifications. Once one understands the delicate commutativity condition one can proceed following Grothendieck footsteps exactly. The square matrices , when viewed up to conjugation, give us new commutative rings with Frobenius endomorphisms.



Introduction

Our starting point is André Weil’s “Roseta - Stone”: The analogy between number fields
and function fields. On the one hand we have the mysterious number fields - the finite
extension of the field of rational numbers
\mathbb{Q}
. On the other hand we have the function
fields: the finite extensions
K/\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
(resp.
K/\mathbb{C}(z)
) of the field of rational
functions
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
(resp. in characteristic
0
:
\mathbb{C}(z)
), which correspond to smooth
projective curves
X_{K}
(with a dominant map
z:X_{K}\to\mathbb{P}_{k}^{1}
,

k=\mathbb{F}_{q}
or
\mathbb{C}
); the points of
X_{K}
correspond to the
valuations of
K/k
.

When we compare the field of rational functions
k(z)
with the field of rational numbers

\mathbb{Q}
we discover:

the “point at infinity”
k\left((\frac{1}{z})\right)\supseteq k\left[|\frac{1}{z}|\right]
k(z)
k((z-\alpha))\equiv k\left[|z-\alpha|\right]\left(\frac{1}{z-\alpha}\right)

\subseteq

\subseteq

k[z]
k[|z-\alpha|]\equiv\lim\limits_{\underset{n}{\leftarrow}}k[z]/(z-\alpha)^{n}

versus:

the “real prime”
\mathbb{R}\supseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}\equiv[-1,1]
\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\equiv\mathbb{Z}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{p}\right)

\subseteq

\subseteq

\mathbb{Z}
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\equiv\lim\limits_{\underset{n}{\leftarrow}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}

The “real integers”
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}
are not closed under addition and so do not
form a Ring. The original Roseta stone contained three different languages talking about
one and the same reality, while in mathematics we have one and the same language - the
language of rings: addition and multiplication - and this language is talking about three
different realities; moreover: it is not the appropriate language -addition is problematic
at the real prime!

1
Bio (perads)

Definition 1.1
.

A Bio (short for Bi-operad) is a pair
\mathscr{P}=(\mathscr{P}^{-},\mathscr{P}^{+})
of symmetric
operads
\mathscr{P}^{-}
and
\mathscr{P}^{+}
“acting” on each other.

Explicitly, we have sets
\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)
,
\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)
,
n\geq 1
, with an action
of the symmetric group
S_{n}
on the right on
\mathscr{P}^{-}
, and on the left on

\mathscr{P}^{+}
,

(1.2)

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

We have
composition
maps

(1.3)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\begin{array}[H]{lll}\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)\times\mathscr{P}^{-}(k_{1})\times\cdots\times\mathscr{P}^{-}(k_{n})\longrightarrow\mathscr{P}^{-}(k_{1}+\cdots+k_{n})\\
(p,p_{1},\cdots,p_{n})\mapsto p\circ(p_{i})\end{array},\\
\\
\begin{array}[H]{l}\mathscr{P}^{+}(k_{1}+\cdots+k_{n})\longleftarrow\mathscr{P}^{+}(k_{1})\times\cdots\times\mathscr{P}^{+}(k_{n})\times\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)\\
(q_{i})\circ q\mapsfrom(q_{1},\cdots,q_{n},q)\end{array}\end{array}

which are associative

(1.4)

(p\circ(p_{i}))\circ(p_{ij})=p\circ\left(p_{i}\circ(p_{ij})\right)\;\;,\;\;\left((q_{ij})\circ q_{i}\right)\circ q=(q_{ij})\circ\left((q_{i})\circ q\right)

and unital

(1.5)

\begin{array}[H]{l}p\circ(1^{-},\cdots,1^{-})=p\;\;,\;\;1^{-}\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(1),\\
\\
\mathscr{P}^{+}(1)\ni 1^{+}\;\;,\;\;q=(1^{+},\cdots,1^{+})\circ q\end{array}

and "
S_{k_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{k_{n}}\rtimes S_{n}\subseteq S_{k_{1}+\ldots+k_{n}}
- covariant." We have also
action
maps

(1.6)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\mathscr{P}^{-}(k_{1}+\cdots+k_{n})\times\mathscr{P}^{+}(k_{1})\times\cdots\times\mathscr{P}^{+}(k_{n})\rightarrow\mathscr{P}^{-}(n),\\
\\
(p,q_{1},\ldots q_{n})\mapsto p\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(q_{i}),\\
\\
\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)\leftarrow\mathscr{P}^{-}(k_{1})\times\cdots\times\mathscr{P}^{-}(k_{n})\times\mathscr{P}^{+}(k_{1}+\cdots+k_{n}),\\
\\
(p_{i})\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,q\mapsfrom(p_{1},\cdots,p_{n},q)\end{array}

which are associative

(1.7)

\begin{array}[H]{l}p\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,\left((q_{ij}\circ q_{i})\right)=\left(p\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(q_{ij})\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(q_{i}),\\
\\
(p_{i})\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,\left((p_{ij})\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,q\right)=\left(p_{i}\circ(p_{ij})\right)\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,q\end{array}

unital

(1.8)

p\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(1^{-})=p\quad,\quad q=(1^{+})\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,q

S_{k_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{k_{n}}
- invariant and
S_{n}
-covariant.

We also require
naturality
of the action maps, so we have

(1.9)

\left(p\circ(p_{i})\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,\left(q_{ij}\right)=p\circ\left(p_{i}\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(q_{ij})\right),

pictorially

p\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(n),p_{i}\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(k_{i1}+\cdots+k_{i,\ell_{i}}),
q_{ij}\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(k_{ij})
p
p_{n}
p_{1}
q_{11}
q_{1\ell_{1}}
q_{n1}
q_{n\ell_{n}}

and symmetrically,

(1.10)

\left((p_{ij})\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,q_{i}\right)\circ q=\left(p_{ij}\right)\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,\left((q_{i})\circ q\right);

We also require co-naturality, so

(1.11)

\big(p\circ(p_{ij})\big)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(q_{i})=p\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,\left((p_{ij})\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,q_{i}\right)

pictorially

p
p_{11}
p_{1\ell_{1}}
p_{n1}
p_{n{\ell_{n}}}
\vdots
\vdots
q_{1}
\vdots
q_{n}
p\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{n}),\;
p_{ij}\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(k_{ij}),\;q_{i}\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(k_{i{1}}+\cdots+k_{i\ell_{i}})

and symmmetrically,

(1.12)

\left(p_{i}\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(q_{ij})\right)\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,q=(p_{i})\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,\left((q_{ij})\circ q\right)

Maps of bios
\varphi:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{Q}
are pairs of maps of symmetric operads

\varphi^{\pm}:\mathscr{P}^{\pm}\to\mathscr{Q}^{\pm}
, preserving the
S_{n}
-actions, the
compositions and units, that also preserve the action maps; thus we have a category Bio.

In this definition we took the operads
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}
to be open. We can take them also to be closed, so that
we have also
\mathscr{P}^{-}(0)=\{0^{-}\}
and
\mathscr{P}^{+}(0)=\{0^{+}\}
, and multiplication by
0^{\pm}
at any place

j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
map
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(n)
to
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(n-1)
.
Similarly we can let
0^{\mp}
act on
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(n)
, at any place
j
, mapping it to
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(n+1)
. With these
0^{\pm}
, and
with an involution (
\mathscr{P}^{-}\xleftrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\mathscr{P}^{+}
), we get a structure equivalent to the one of
[
Har17
]
.

Remark 1.13
.

The monoids
\mathscr{P}^{-}(1)
and
\mathscr{P}^{+}(1)
are connonically isomorphic via

\mathscr{P}^{-}(1)\xleftrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathscr{P}^{+}(1)

f^{-}\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt}f^{-}\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,1^{+}

We use this as an identification: It identifies
1^{-}
with
1^{+}
, and for

p\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)
,
q\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)
,
p\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,q
is identified with

p\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,q
.

We will abuse notations and write
\circ
for either

\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,
or
\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,
.

Example 1.14
.

Given a symmetric monoidal category
\mathcal{E}
and an object
X\in\mathcal{E}^{\circ}
, we have
the bio
\End_{\mathcal{E}}(X)
with

\End_{\mathcal{E}}^{-}(X)(n)=\mathcal{E}(X^{\otimes n},X)\quad,\quad\End_{\mathcal{E}}^{+}(X)(n)\equiv\mathcal{E}(X,X^{\otimes n})

with all the compositions and actions given by the monoidal structure of

\mathcal{E}
.

Remark 1.15
.

Given a symmetric monoidal category
\mathcal{E}
, we can replace Set everywhere by

\mathcal{E}
and obtain the category
\bio(\mathcal{E})
of bios in
\mathcal{E}
.

Remark 1.16
.

The category
\bio
has an involution

\mathscr{P}=(\mathscr{P}^{-},\mathscr{P}^{+})\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\left((\mathscr{P}^{\text{op}})^{-}\equiv\mathscr{P}^{+},(\mathscr{P}^{\text{op}})^{+}\equiv\mathscr{P}^{-}\right).

It takes the monoid
\mathscr{P}(1)
to the opposite monoid

\mathscr{P}^{\circ p}(1)\equiv\mathscr{P}(1)^{\text{op}}
.

We get the category
\bio^{t}
, with
objects the bios
\mathscr{P}
with an involution

p\mapsto p^{t}:\mathscr{P}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\quad}\mathscr{P}^{\text{op}},

and with maps preserving the involution.

2
Rigs

Definition 2.1
.

A
Rig
(
\equiv
Ring without Negatives) is a set
R
with two
operations of addition and multiplication

(2.2)

R\times R\rightrightarrows R\quad,\quad(x,y)\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}\begin{array}[H]{l}x+y\\
x\cdot y\end{array},

both associative and unital

(2.3)

\begin{array}[H]{lll}(x+y)+z=x+(y+z)\quad,\quad(x\cdot y)\cdot z=x\cdot(y\cdot z)\\
\\
x+0=x=0+x\quad,\quad x\cdot 1=x=1\cdot x\end{array}

with addition always commutative
x+y=y+x
, and distributive

(2.4)

\begin{array}[H]{l}(x_{1}+x_{2})\cdot y=(x_{1}\cdot y)+(x_{2}\cdot y)\quad,\quad x\cdot(y_{1}+y_{2})=(x\cdot y_{1})+(x\cdot y_{2})\\
\\
x\cdot 0=0=0\cdot x\end{array}

A rig with involution is a rig
R
with an involution

(2.5)

\begin{array}[H]{l}x\mapsto x^{t}:R\righttoleftarrow\quad,\quad\text{satisfying}\;x^{tt}=x\;\text{and}\quad 0^{t}=0\quad,\quad 1^{t}=1\\
\\
(x+y)^{t}=x^{t}+y^{t}\quad,\quad(x\cdot y)^{t}=y^{t}\cdot x^{t}\end{array}

A rig is commutative
R\in C\text{Rig}
if multiplication is commutative,

x\cdot y=y\cdot x

A map of rigs
\varphi:R\to R^{\prime}
is a set map preserving the operations and
constants

(2.6)

\varphi(0)=0\quad,\quad\varphi(1)=1\quad,\quad\varphi(x+y)=\varphi(x)+\varphi(y)\quad,\quad\varphi(x\cdot y)=\varphi(x)\cdot\varphi(y)

and in the self-dual case
\text{Rig}^{t}
,
\varphi
should also preserve the involution,

\varphi(x^{t})=\varphi(x)^{t}.

Thus we have categories and functors

\displaystyle\begin{array}[H]{l}\hbox to121.83pt{\vbox to62.84pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-15.78987pt\lower 34.95724pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{19.12288pt}{82.36325pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{106.24156pt}{82.91383pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{

(for a commutative rig the identity is an involution).

There is a similar diagram with
Ring instead of Rig. The inclusion
\text{Ring}\hookrightarrow\text{Rig}
has the left
adjoint functor
K=\text{Grothendieck functor localizing addition}
.

E.g. We have the commutative
rigs

(2.8)

\mathscr{B}=\left\{0,1\right\}\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;\mathscr{R}=[0,\infty]

with the usual multiplication
x\cdot y
, and with the “tropical” addition

(2.9)

x+y:\overset{def}{=}\max\left\{x,y\right\}.

For
A\in\text{Rig}
we have the operad
\mathscr{P}_{A}

with
\mathscr{P}_{A}^{-}(n)\equiv A^{n}
, and with compositions

(2.10)

o_{i}:A^{n}\times A^{m}\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}A^{n+m-1}

(a_{1},\cdots,a_{i},\cdots a_{n})\circ_{i}(b_{1},\cdots,b_{m}):=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{i-1},a_{i}\cdot b_{1},a_{i}\cdot b_{2}\cdots a_{i}\cdot b_{m},a_{i+1}\cdots a_{m})

We have similarly the operad
\mathscr{P}_{A}^{+}\equiv\mathscr{P}_{A^{\text{op}}}
of column vectors

\mathscr{P}_{A}^{+}(n)\equiv A^{n}
, with composition

(2.11)

\begin{array}[H]{c}A^{n}\times A^{m}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}A^{m+n-1},\;\;\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\\
\vdots\\
a_{n}\end{pmatrix}\circ_{j}\begin{pmatrix}b_{1}\\
\vdots\\
b_{j}\\
\vdots\\
b_{m}\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}b_{1}\\
\vdots\\
a_{1}\cdot b_{j}\\
\vdots\\
a_{n}\cdot b_{j}\\
\vdots\\
b_{m}\end{pmatrix}\end{array}

Moreover, we have mutual actions, for
1\leq k\leq m\leq n
,

(2.12)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\overleftarrow{\circ}_{k}:A^{n}\times A^{m-k+1}\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}A^{n-m+k}\\
\\
\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\cdots a_{k}\cdots a_{m}\cdots a_{n}\end{pmatrix}\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,_{k}\begin{pmatrix}b_{0}\\
b_{1}\\
\vdots\\
b_{m-k}\end{pmatrix}:=\\
\begin{pmatrix}a_{1},\cdots a_{k-1},a_{k}\cdot b_{0}+\cdots+a_{k+j}\cdot b_{j}+\cdots+a_{m}\cdot b_{m-k},a_{m+1},\cdots a_{n}\end{pmatrix}\end{array}

and similarly for
\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,
.

This construction gives a full and faithful embedding

(2.13)

\text{Rig}\;\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.92 0 L 38.82 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{5.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.87 C 3.67 3.87 6.64 3.01 6.64 1.94 C 6.64 0.87 3.67 0 0 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{28.25276pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;\text{Bio}\quad,\quad A\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathscr{P}_{A}.

Moreover, when the rig
A
has an involution, the bio
\mathscr{P}_{A}=\left(\mathscr{P}^{-}_{A},\mathscr{P}^{+}_{A}\right)
has an involution

(2.14)

(a_{1},\cdots,a_{n})^{t}:=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}^{t}\\
\vdots\\
a_{n}^{t}\end{pmatrix}

giving

(2.15)

\text{Rig}^{t}\hbox to57.31pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.92 0 L 78.19 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{5.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.87 C 3.67 3.87 6.64 3.01 6.64 1.94 C 6.64 0.87 3.67 0 0 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{56.70552pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;\text{Bio}^{t}

Remark 2.16
.

For any rig
A
the associated bio
\mathscr{P}_{A}
contains the sub-bio
\mathbb{F}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{A}
with

(2.17)

\begin{array}[H]{ll}\mathbb{F}^{-}(n)&=\left\{0=(0,\cdots,0),\delta_{1}=(1,0,\cdots,0),\cdots,\delta_{n}=(0,\cdots 0,1)\right\}\\
\\
\mathbb{F}^{+}(n)&=\left\{0^{t}=\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{matrix}\right),\delta_{1}^{t}=\left(\begin{matrix}1\\
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{matrix}\right),\cdots,\delta_{n}^{t}=\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
\vdots\\
0\\
1\end{matrix}\right)\right\}\end{array}

the (co)-vectors with at most one
1
coordinate (note that
\mathbb{F}
is closed
under the composition and action operations); we call
\mathbb{F}

“
the field with one element
”.

3
The
\ell_{p}
bios and the real and complex “integers”

Fix
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, with
1/p+1/q=1
.

We have the operad

\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{p}}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{-}
, with

(3.1)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{p}}(n):=\left\{(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}(n)\equiv\mathbb{R}^{n},|x_{1}|^{p}+\cdots+|x_{n}|^{p}\leq 1\right\}\\
\\
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\circ_{i}(y_{1},\cdots,y_{m}):=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{i-1},x_{i}\cdot y_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\cdot y_{m},x_{i+1},\cdots,x_{n})\end{array}

We write the elements of
\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{q}}
as column vector, and we have the mutual actions
induced from
\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
,

(3.2)

(a_{1}\cdots a_{k}\cdots a_{n})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,_{k}\begin{pmatrix}b_{0}\\
b_{1}\\
\vdots\\
b_{m-k}\end{pmatrix}:=(a_{1}\cdots a_{k-1},a_{k}\cdot b_{0}+\cdots+a_{k+j}\cdot b_{j}+\cdots+a_{m}\cdot b_{m-k},a_{m+1},\cdots,a_{n})

and similarly for
\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,
. Thus we have a
\text{sub}\text{-bio}\,(\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{p}},\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{q}})\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

The rig
\mathbb{R}
, being commutative has an (identity) involution, hence
\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
has an
involution

(3.3)

(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})^{t}:=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\\
\vdots\\
a_{n}\end{pmatrix}

The sub-bio
\left(\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{p}},\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{q}}\right)\subseteq\left(\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{-},\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\right)
is stable under this involution iff
p=q=2
, and we are in the self-dual case

(3.4)

\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{2}}(n)\equiv\left\{(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n},x_{1}^{2}+\cdots+x_{n}^{2}\leq 1\right\}

the
\ell_{2}
-unit ball.

The bio
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}:=(\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{2}},\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{2}})\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
is the “Real integers”,
analogue at the “Real prime” of the
p
-adic integers
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
p
a prime. We similarly have the “complex integers”
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{C}}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
given by the unit
\ell_{2}
complex balls.

Contracting the inside of the unit
\ell_{2}
-balls to a point we get quotient bios

(3.5)

\begin{array}[H]{lll}\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{F}_{\mathbb{R}}&,&\mathbb{F}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\pm}(n)\equiv S^{n-1}\sqcup\left\{0\right\}\\
\\
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{C}}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{F}_{\mathbb{C}}&,&\mathbb{F}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\pm}(n)\equiv S^{2n-1}\sqcup\left\{0\right\}\end{array}

We now have a complete picture of the arithmetical bios

\displaystyle\begin{array}[H]{l}\hbox to187.37pt{\vbox to101.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-11.61801pt\lower 5.74062pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

4
Commutativity

We say the bio
\mathscr{P}=(\mathscr{P}^{-},\mathscr{P}^{+})
is
commutative
if the actions and
compositions
interchange
:
for
\tilde{p}\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(\ell)
,
q\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(m)
,

p\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)
,

(4.1)

\left(\tilde{p}\overleftarrow{\circ}_{\hskip-2.84526ptj}\,q\right){\circ}_{j}p\equiv(\tilde{p}\circ\underbrace{(p,\cdots,p)}_{m})\sigma_{m,n}\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,\underbrace{\left(q,\cdots,q\right)}_{n}

and similarly,

(4.2)

q\circ_{j}\left(p\overrightarrow{\circ}_{\hskip-2.84526ptj}\,\tilde{q}\right)\equiv(\underbrace{p,\cdots,p}_{n})\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,\sigma_{n,m}((\underbrace{q,\cdots,q}_{m})\circ\tilde{q})

for
\tilde{q}\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(\ell)
,
p\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(m)
,
q\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)
.

Pictorially,

We let
\text{CBio}\subseteq\text{Bio}
denote the full subcategory of
commutative bios.

For a commutative bio
\mathscr{P}
the underlying (associative, unital) monoid
\mathscr{P}(1)
is
commutative. Moreover,
\mathscr{P}(1)
acts
centrally
on
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(n)
: for
a\in\mathscr{P}(1)

(4.4)

a\circ p=p\circ(a,\cdots,a)\;\;,\;\;p\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)\;\;;\;\;q\circ a=(a,\cdots a)\circ q\;\;,\;\;q\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)

For a
multiplicative subset

S\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)

(4.5)

s_{1},s_{2}\in S\Longrightarrow s_{1}\circ s_{2}\in S,\;\;{\mathbf{1}}\in S,

we have the
localization
of
\mathscr{P}
at
S
,
S^{-1}\mathscr{P}
:

(4.6)

\begin{array}[H]{l}S^{-1}\mathscr{P}\equiv\mathscr{P}[S^{-1}]\equiv(S^{-1}\mathscr{P}^{-},S^{-1}\mathscr{P}^{+})\\
\\
S^{-1}\mathscr{P}^{\pm}=\mathscr{P}^{\pm}\times S/\sim\end{array}

with
(P^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime})\sim(P^{\prime},s^{\prime})
iff
s\circ s^{\prime}\circ P^{\prime\prime}=s\circ s^{\prime\prime}\circ P^{\prime}
for some
s\in S
; one writes

P/s=\frac{1}{s}\circ P
for the equivalence class
(P,s)/\sim
.

For a commutative rig

A
the bio
\mathscr{P}_{A}
is commutative; this is true in particular for commutative
rings
A
, and we shall continue and define ideals, primes, spectrum, schemes etc. for
commutative bios making sure that we get the right definitions for

\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}_{A}
,
A\in C\text{Ring}
.

Remark 4.7
.

We denote by
\ctbio\subseteq\cbio
the full sub-categorys consisting of the
“totally-commutative” bios
\mathscr{P}=(\mathscr{P}^{-},\mathscr{P}^{+})
, which, in addition to
commutativity, satisfy the Boardman-Vogt interchange: For
p\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)
,

p^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(m)
,

p\circ(\underbrace{p^{\prime},\cdots,p^{\prime}}_{n})=p^{\prime}\circ(\underbrace{p,\cdots p}_{m})\circ\sigma_{m,n}

and symmetrically, for
q\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)
,
q^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(m)
,

(\underbrace{q^{\prime},\cdots,q^{\prime}}_{n})\circ q=\sigma_{n,m}\circ(\underbrace{q,\cdots q}_{m})\circ q^{\prime}

We have the full subcategories

(4.8)

\text{CRing}\subseteq\crig\subseteq\ctbio\subseteq\cbio\subseteq\bio

They are all complete and co-complete. All limits are created in Sets. Also directed co-limits
are created in Sets. These categories also have all co-limits, and in particular we have
push-outs for a diagram

B
A_{1}
A_{0}

which will be denoted respectively by

(4.9)

A_{0}\underset{B}{\otimes}A_{1},\quad A_{0}\underset{B}{\otimes}A_{1},\quad A_{0}\underset{B}{\boxtimes}^{T}A_{1},\quad A_{0}\underset{B}{\boxtimes}A_{1},\quad A_{0}\underset{B}{\amalg}A_{1}\quad

for the categories of (
4.8
). We have

(4.10)

A_{0}\boxtimes_{B}A_{1}=\left(A_{0}\underset{B}{\amalg}A_{1}\right)^{C}\quad,\quad A_{0}\underset{B}{\boxtimes}^{T}A_{1}=\left(A_{0}\underset{B}{\boxtimes}A_{1}\right)^{T},

where

(4.11)

A\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow A^{C}\quad,\text{resp.}\;\;A\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow A^{T},

is the maximal (resp. totally) commutative quotient of
A
giving the left adjoint of the
embedding
\cbio\subseteq\bio
, resp.
\ctbio\subseteq\cbio
.

Remark 4.12
.

For (the initial object)
\mathbb{Z}\in C\text{Ring}\subseteq C\text{Rig}
, the
“arithmetical surface”

\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}\right)\equiv\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}\right)\prod\limits_{\text{spec}(\mathbb{F})}\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}\right)

does not reduce to its diagonal, as it does in classical algebraic geometry where

\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})\prod\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})=\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z}\otimes\mathbb{Z})=\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})=\left\{(0);(2),(3),(5),(7)\cdots\right\}.

Taking
B=\mathbb{F}
,

A_{i}=\mathbb{Z}
, in (
4.9
) we get

\mathbb{Z}\underset{\mathbb{F}}{\amalg}\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\;\;\;}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{F}}\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\;\;\;}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{F}}^{T}\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\;\overset{\nabla}{\sim}\;}\mathbb{Z}.

If we require total-commutativity the arithmetical surface again reduces to its
diagonal, and
\mathbb{Z}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{F}}^{T}\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{Z}
. Indeed,
\mathbb{Z}
is generated over

\mathbb{F}
by
v=(1,1)
,
v^{t}=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
1\end{pmatrix}
, (and
(-1)
), and total commutativity implies

(4.13)

\begin{array}[H]{ll}(v\boxtimes 1)\,\circ&(1\boxtimes v,1\boxtimes v)\circ\big((1,0),(0,1)\big)=(1\boxtimes v)\circ(v\boxtimes 1,v\boxtimes 1)\circ\big((1,0),(0,1)\big)\\
\\
&\implies v\boxtimes 1=1\boxtimes v\end{array}

pictorially

(4.14)

5
The spectrum

We have the categories of “
bios with zero
”

(5.1)

\phantom{*}{}_{\mathbb{F}}\backslash\cbio\subseteq\cbio(\text{Set}_{0})\subseteq\cbio_{0}\subseteq\cbio

where
\mathscr{P}\in\cbio_{0}
iff there exist
0\in\mathscr{P}(1)
with
0\circ x=0
for all

x\in\mathscr{P}(1)
; such an element
0
is unique; the maps
\varphi
in
\cbio_{0}
are required
to preserve it:
\varphi(0)=0
.
Fix
\mathscr{P}=(\mathscr{P}^{-},\mathscr{P}^{+})\in\text{CBio}_{0}
.

Definition 5.2
.

An
ideal
is a subset
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)
such that for

a_{1}\cdots a_{n}\in\mathfrak{a}
,
b\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(n)
,
d\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(n)
, the

“linear combination”

(5.3)

\left\{b,(a_{i}),d\right\}\overset{\text{def}}{=}\left(b\circ(a_{i})\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d=b\,\overrightarrow{\circ}\,\left((a_{i})\circ d\right)\in\mathfrak{a}\;\;\;\text{is in $\mathfrak{a}$}

Note that intersection of ideals is again an ideal, so we can speak of the ideal
\mathfrak{a}

generated by a set
\left\{a_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1);\mathfrak{a}
is also given as
the collection of all linear - combinations of the
a_{i}
’s (with repetition).

A proper ideal
\mathfrak{p}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)
, with
1\not\in\mathfrak{p}
, is called
prime
if

S_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\mathfrak{p}
is multiplicative. The set of primes is denoted

\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
.

The set
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
is not empty: a maximal proper ideal
1\not\in\mathfrak{p}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)
, which exists by Zorn’s lemma, is always prime. Indeed, if

a,a^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\mathfrak{p}
, then for some linear combinations

\left\{b,(a_{i})_{i\leq n},d\right\}={\mathbf{1}}=\left\{b^{\prime},(a^{\prime}_{j})_{j\leq m},d^{\prime}\right\},b,b^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{-},d,d^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{+},

a_{i}\in\mathfrak{p}
or
a_{i}=a
,
a_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{p}
or

a_{j}^{\prime}=a^{\prime}
and by commutativity we have

\begin{array}[H]{lll}1=1\circ 1&=&\left(\left(b\circ(a_{i})\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d\right)\circ\left(\left(b^{\prime}\circ(a_{j}^{\prime})\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d^{\prime}\right)\\
\\
&=&\left(b\circ(\underbrace{b^{\prime},\cdots b^{\prime}}_{n})\right)\sigma\circ(a_{i}\circ a_{j}^{\prime})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,\sigma^{\prime}\left((\underbrace{d,\cdots,d}_{m})\circ d^{\prime}\right)\end{array}

with
a_{i}\circ a_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{p}
or
a_{i}\circ a_{j}^{\prime}=a\circ a^{\prime}
,
and so
a\circ a^{\prime}\not\in\mathfrak{p}
.

Moreover,
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
is a (compact,
sober
\equiv
Zariski) topological space with respect to the
Zariski topology
with
closed sets

(5.4)

\begin{array}[H]{l}V(\mathfrak{a}):=\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}),\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{a}\right\}\quad,\quad\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)\;\text{ideal},\\
\\
V(\mathfrak{a})\cap V(\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})=V(\mathfrak{a}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})\quad,\quad\mathfrak{a}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}=\text{the ideal generated by $\left\{a\cdot a^{\prime},a\in\mathfrak{a},a^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}\right\}$};\\
\\
\bigcup\limits_{i}V(\mathfrak{a}_{i})=V(\sum\limits_{i}\mathfrak{a}_{i})\quad,\quad\sum\limits_{i}\mathfrak{a}_{i}\;\;\text{the ideal generated by
$\cup_{i}\mathfrak{a}_{i}$};\\
\\
V(0)=\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\quad,\quad V(1)=\phi\end{array}

We have a basis for the topology by
basic open sets

(5.5)

\begin{array}[H]{c}D(f):=\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}),\mathfrak{p}\not\ni f\right\}\quad,\quad f\in\mathscr{P}(1).\\
\\
D(f_{1})\cap D(f_{2})=D\left(f_{1}\circ f_{2}\right)\quad,\quad D(1)=\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\quad,\quad D(0)=\phi,\\
\\
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\setminus V(\mathfrak{a})=\bigcup_{f\in\mathfrak{a}}D(f)\end{array}

For a map of commutative bios
\varphi\in\text{CBio}(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q})
, the pull back of a
(prime) ideal is a (prime) ideal, and we obtain

(5.6)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\text{spec}(\varphi)=\varphi^{\ast}:\text{spec}(\mathscr{Q})\longrightarrow\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\\
\\
\varphi^{\ast}(\mathfrak{q})=\varphi_{1}^{-1}(\mathfrak{q})=\left\{a\in\mathscr{P}(1),\varphi(a)\in\mathfrak{q}\right\}.\end{array}

It is a continuous map:

(5.7)

\varphi^{\ast^{-1}}\big(V(\mathfrak{a})\big)=\left\{\mathfrak{q}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{Q}),\varphi^{-1}(\mathfrak{q})\supseteq\mathfrak{a}\right\}=V\big(\varphi(\mathfrak{a})\big)

and similarly the pull back of basic open set is again basic open

(5.8)

\varphi^{\ast^{-1}}\left(D(f)\right)=\left\{\mathfrak{q}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{Q}),\varphi^{-1}(\mathfrak{q})\not\ni f\right\}=D\big(\varphi(f)\big).

Thus we have a functor

(5.9)

\text{spec}:(\text{CBio}_{0})^{\text{op}}\longrightarrow\text{Top}\;\text{(compact, sober)}

We have a Galois correspondence (
\equiv
adjunction of order sets)

(5.10)

\left\{\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathscr{P}(1)\;\text{ideal}\right\}
V
I
\left\{Z\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\;\text{closed}\right\}

\bigcap\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in Z}\mathfrak{p}=:I(Z)\;\hbox to57.31pt{\vbox to5.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{{
{
\lxSVG@setdash{0.0pt}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@buttcap\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@miterjoin\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}

6
The structure sheaf
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}

Fix
\mathscr{P}\in\text{CBio}_{0}
. For an open set
\mathscr{U}\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
define

(6.1)

\mathscr{O}^{\pm}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathscr{U})(n):=\left\{f:\mathscr{U}\to\coprod\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathscr{U}}\mathscr{P}^{\pm}_{\mathfrak{p}}(n),f(\mathfrak{p})\in\mathscr{P}^{\pm}_{\mathfrak{p}}(n),\;\text{and $f$ is a
{\it{\bf{local fraction}}}}\right\}

f

locally a fraction
: for all
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathscr{U}
, there exists open

\mathfrak{p}\in\mathscr{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq\mathscr{U}
, and
P\in\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(n)
,
s\in\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\bigcup\limits_{\mathfrak{q}\in\mathscr{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathfrak{q}
, such that for all

\mathfrak{q}\in\mathscr{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
we have
f(\mathfrak{q})\equiv P/s
in
\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Note that
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathscr{U})=\left(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}^{-}(\mathscr{U}),\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}^{+}(\mathscr{U})\right)
is a
commutative bio, the operations of compositions and actions are defined
pointwise (in each
\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}
), and the
“local fraction condition” is preserved. For open sets
\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathscr{U}\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
we have the restriction maps

(6.2)

\rho_{\mathscr{V}}^{\mathscr{U}}:\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U})\to\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{V})

making
\mathscr{U}\mapsto\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U})
a pre-sheaf of bios over
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
; by the local
nature of the “locally-fraction-condition” it is clearly a sheaf.

For
\mathfrak{p}\in\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
, the
stalk
of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}
at

\mathfrak{p}
is given by

(6.3)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P},\mathfrak{p}}:=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{\mathscr{U}\ni\mathfrak{p}}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathscr{U})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}\\
\\
(\mathscr{U},f)_{/\approx}\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}f(\mathfrak{p})\end{array}

it is well defined, surjective, and injective.

The global sections of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}
are given by

Theorem 1
.

For a basic open set
D(s)\subseteq\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
,
s\in\mathscr{P}(1)
,

\begin{array}[H]{c}\Psi:\mathscr{P}_{s}=\mathscr{P}\left[\frac{1}{s}\right]\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}\left(D(s)\right)\\
\\
\Psi\left(P/s^{n}\right):=\left\{f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P/s^{n}\;\text{in
$\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}$ for all $\mathfrak{p}\in D(s)$}\right\}.\end{array}

For
s=1
we obtain the global sections

\mathscr{P}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}(\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}))

Proof.

The map
\Psi
which takes
P/s^{n}\in\mathscr{P}_{s}
to the constant section

f
,
f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P/s^{n}
, is a well defined map of bios. Take
P\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(k)
, the case
P\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(k)
is similar.

\Psi
is injective
: Assume
\Psi\left(P_{1}/s^{n_{1}}\right)=\Psi(P_{2}/s^{n_{2}})
, and define

\mathfrak{a}:=\text{Ann}\left(s^{n_{2}}\circ P_{1};s^{n_{1}}\circ P_{2}\right)=\left\{a\in\mathscr{P}(1),a\circ s^{n_{2}}\circ P_{1}=a\circ s^{n_{1}}\circ P_{2}\right\};

by commutativity it follows
\mathfrak{a}
is an ideal. We have

P_{1}/s^{n_{1}}=P_{2}/s^{n_{2}}\;\;\text{in}\;\;\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\;\text{for all}\;\;\mathfrak{p}\in D(s)

\Rightarrow

s_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ s^{n_{2}}\circ P_{1}=s_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ s^{n_{1}}\circ P_{2}
for some
s_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\mathfrak{p}
,
all
\mathfrak{p}\in D(s)
.

\Rightarrow

\mathfrak{a}\not\subseteq\mathfrak{p}
, all

\mathfrak{p}\in D(s)

\Rightarrow

V(\mathfrak{a})\cap D(s)=\emptyset\Rightarrow V(\mathfrak{a})\subseteq V(s)\Rightarrow s\in IV(\mathfrak{a})=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}}

\Rightarrow

s^{n}\in\mathfrak{a}
for
n>>0\Rightarrow s^{n+n_{2}}\circ P_{1}=s^{n+n_{1}}\circ P_{2}
for
n>>0

\Rightarrow

P_{1}/s^{n_{1}}=P_{2}/s^{n_{2}}
in
\mathscr{P}_{s}
.

\Psi
is surjective
: Fix
f\in\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}(D(s))^{-}(k)
. Since
D(s)
is compact we can cover it by
finite collection of basic open sets

D(s)=D(s_{1})\cup\cdots\cup D(s_{N})

with

f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P_{i}/t_{i}\;\text{for}\;\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i})\quad;\quad t_{i}\in\mathscr{P}(1)\setminus\bigcup\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i})}\mathfrak{p}.

We have
V(t_{i})\subseteq V(s_{i})
, so
s_{i}^{n_{i}}=c_{i}\circ t_{i}
for some

c_{i}\in\mathscr{P}(1)
, and for
\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i})
,
f(\mathfrak{p})=P_{i}/t_{i}=c_{i}\circ P_{i}/s_{i}^{n_{i}}
. We can replace
s_{i}
by
s_{i}^{n_{i}}
,
(
D(s_{i})=D(s_{i}^{n_{i}})
), and
P_{i}
by
c_{i}\circ P_{i}
, so

f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P_{i}/s_{i}\quad\text{for}\;\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i}).

On the set
D(s_{i})\cap D(s_{j})=D(s_{i}\circ s_{j})
, the section
f
is given
by both
P_{i}/s_{i}
and
P_{j}/s_{j}
, and by the injectivity of
\Psi

(s_{i}\circ s_{j})^{n}\circ s_{j}\circ P_{i}=(s_{i}\circ s_{j})^{n}\circ s_{i}\circ P_{j}

By finiteness we may assume one
n
works for all
i,j
.

Replacing
s_{i}
by

s_{i}^{n+1}
, and replacing
s_{i}^{n}\circ P_{i}
by
P_{i}
, we may assume

\begin{array}[H]{l}f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P_{i}/s_{i}\quad\text{for all
$\mathfrak{p}\in D(s_{i})$}\\
\\
s_{j}\circ P_{i}=s_{i}\circ P_{j}\quad\text{all $i,j$.}\end{array}

Since
D(s)\subseteq\bigcup\limits_{i}D(s_{i})
we have that some power

s^{M}
is a linear-combination of the
s_{i}

\begin{array}[H]{l}s^{M}=\left\{b,(c_{j}),d\right\}=b\circ(c_{j})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d\quad,\quad c_{j}=s_{i(j)}\quad,\quad b,d\in\mathscr{P}^{\pm}(\ell)\quad,\\
\\
j\in\left\{1,\cdots,\ell\right\}\xrightarrow{i}\left\{1,\cdots,N\right\}.\end{array}

Define
P=b\circ(P_{i(j)})\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(\underbrace{d,\ldots,d}_{k})
.

We have,

\begin{array}[H]{lll}s_{j}\circ P&=&b\circ\left(s_{j}\circ P_{i(j)}\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(d,\cdots,d)\equiv b\circ\left(s_{i(j)}\circ P_{j}\right)\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(d,\cdots,d)\\
\\
&=&(b\circ(c_{j}\circ P_{j}))\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,(d,\cdots,d)\\
\\
&=&((b\circ(c_{j}))\,\overleftarrow{\circ}\,d)\circ P_{j}\qquad\text{by commutativity}\\
\\
&=&s^{M}\circ P_{j}\end{array}

Thus
f(\mathfrak{p})\equiv P_{j}/s_{j}\equiv P/s^{M}
is constant.
∎

7
Generalized Schemes

For a topological space
\mathcal{X}
, we have the category
\text{CBio}/_{\mathcal{X}}
of
sheaves of
\text{CBio}_{0}
over
\mathcal{X}
, its maps are natural transformations

\varphi=\left\{\varphi_{\mathscr{U}}\right\}
,
\varphi_{\mathscr{U}}\in\text{CBio}_{0}(\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U}),\mathscr{P}^{\prime}(\mathscr{U}))
. Putting all these categories together we have
the category
\text{CBio}/\text{Top}
: its object are pairs
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P})
,

\mathcal{X}\in\text{Top}
,
\mathscr{P}\in\text{CBio}/\mathcal{X}
, and its maps

f:(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P})\to(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})
are pairs
f\in\text{Top}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
and
f^{\natural}\in\text{CBio}/_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}(\mathscr{P}^{\prime},f_{\ast}\mathscr{P})
; explicitly,
f
is a
continuous function, and for
\mathscr{U}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
open, we have the map of bios

f_{\mathscr{U}}^{\natural}\in\text{CBio}_{0}\left(\mathscr{P}^{\prime}(\mathscr{U}),\mathscr{P}\left(f^{-1}(\mathscr{U})\right)\right)
,
these maps being compatible with restrictions.

Remark 7.1
.

For
f\in\text{Top}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
we have adjunction

\text{CBio}/\mathcal{X}
\text{CBio}/\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
f^{\ast}
f_{\ast}
f^{\ast}\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=
sheaf associated
f_{\ast}\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U})=\mathscr{P}(f^{-1}\mathscr{U})

to the pre-sheaf
\hskip-170.71652pt\mathcal{X}\supseteq\mathscr{V}\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}\hskip-28.45274pt\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{\begin{array}[H]{l}\hskip 28.45274pt\mathscr{U}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\,\text{open}\\
\hskip 28.45274ptf(\mathscr{V})\subseteq\mathscr{U}\end{array}}\hskip-19.91692pt\mathscr{P}^{\prime}(\mathscr{U})

For a map
f\in\text{CBio}/{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)
, and for a point
x\in\mathcal{X}
, we get an induced map of stalks

(7.2)

f_{x}^{\natural}:\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{f(x)}=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{f(x)\in\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathscr{P}^{\prime}(\mathscr{V})\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{f(x)\in\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathscr{P}(f^{-1}\mathscr{V})\to\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{x\in\mathscr{U}\subseteq\mathcal{X}}\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U})=\mathscr{P}_{x}

Definition 7.3
.

The category of
locally-bio-spaces

\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}
, is the category with object

(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P})\in\text{CBio}/_{\text{Top}}
, such that for all
x\in\mathcal{X}

the stalk
\mathscr{P}_{x}
is a
local
bio with the unique maximal proper ideal

m_{x}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{x}(1)
; the maps

f\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)
are maps
f\in\text{CBio}/_{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)
such that for all
x\in X
,
f^{\natural}_{x}\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}\left(\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{f(x)},\mathscr{P}_{x}\right)
is a
local
map:

f_{x}^{\natural}(m_{f(x)})\subseteq m_{x}
.

Theorem 2
.

We have the adjunction

\left(\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}\right)^{\text{op}}
\text{CBio}_{0}
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}):=\left(\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}),\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{P}}\right)
spec
\Gamma

\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}):=\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})
the global sections

\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}(\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)\equiv\text{CBio}_{0}\left(\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)

Proof.

For a point
x\in\mathcal{X}
, we have canonical map
\phi_{x}\in\text{CBio}_{0}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X}),\mathscr{P}_{x}\right)
,
\phi_{x}P=P|_{x}
the stalk of the global
section
P
at the point
x
, and we get a prime

\mathfrak{p}_{x}:=\phi_{x}^{-1}(m_{x})\in\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
,
m_{x}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{x}(1)
the maximal ideal. For a basic open set
D(s)\subseteq\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
,
s\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})(1)
, we have
\left\{x\in X,\mathfrak{p}_{x}\in D(s)\right\}=\left\{x\in X,\phi_{x}(s)\not\in m_{x}\right\}
is
open
in

\mathcal{X}
: if
\phi_{x}(s)\not\in m_{x}
, it is invertible in
\mathscr{P}_{x}
,

a_{x}\circ\phi_{x}(s)=1
,
a_{x}\in\mathscr{P}_{x}
and there is an open

\mathscr{U}_{x}\ni x
,

a\in\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U}_{x})
with
a_{x}=a|_{x}
; taking
\mathscr{U}_{x}
smaller we have
a\circ s|_{\mathscr{U}_{x}}=1
already in
\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{U}_{x})
, and so
\phi_{x^{\prime}}(s)\not\in m_{x^{\prime}}
for all
x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{U}_{x}
. Thus the map

x\mapsto\mathfrak{p}_{x}
is a continuous map

\mathfrak{p}:\mathcal{X}\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
.
The uniqueness of the inverse
\left(s|_{u_{x}}\right)^{-1}
, shows these
local inverses glue to a global inverse
s^{-1}\in\mathscr{P}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\left(D(s)\right)\right)
and we get the map of
\text{CBio}_{0}

(7.4)

\mathfrak{p}_{D(s)}^{\natural}:\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})_{s}\equiv\left\{s^{\mathbb{N}}\right\}^{-1}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathscr{P}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\left(D(s)\right)\right)

These maps are compatible on intersections,
D(s_{1})\cap D(s_{2})=D(s_{1}\circ s_{2})
, so by the sheaf property give
\mathfrak{p}_{\mathscr{U}}^{\natural}\in\text{CBio}_{0}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\text{spec}\;\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})}(\mathscr{U}),\mathscr{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\mathscr{U})\right)
for any open
\mathscr{U}\subseteq\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
,
compatible with restrictions, so

(7.5)

\mathfrak{p}=(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}^{\natural})\in\text{CBio}/_{\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)\right)

For
x\in\mathcal{X}
, we get

(7.6)

\mathfrak{p}_{x}^{\natural}=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\text{lim}}}\limits_{\phi_{x}(s)\not\in m_{x}}\mathfrak{p}^{\natural}_{D(s)}\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})_{\mathfrak{p}_{x}},\mathscr{P}_{x}\right)

is
local
, so
\mathfrak{p}\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}/\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)\right)

is the co-unit of adjunction.

Given
\varphi\in\text{CBio}_{0}\left(A,\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})\right)
we get
\text{spec}(\varphi)\circ\mathfrak{p}\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}/\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}(A)\right)
.

Given
f\in\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}/\text{Top}}\left((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}),\text{spec}(A)\right)
we get the global sections

(7.7)

\Gamma(f)=f_{\text{spec}(A)}^{\natural}\in\text{CBio}_{0}(A,\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{X})).

One checks these are inverse bijections.
∎

Following the footsteps of Grothendieck
we can define the category of
Generalized Schemes

\text{GSch}\subseteq\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}
to be the full subcategory of

\text{CBio}_{\text{loc}}/_{\text{Top}}
consisting of the object

(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
which are locally affine: we have some open
cover
\mathcal{X}=\bigcup\mathscr{U}_{i}
, and
(\mathscr{U}_{i},\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathscr{U}_{i}})\cong\text{spec}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathscr{U}_{i})\right)
.

An open subset of a scheme is again a scheme.

Schemes can be glued along open subsets and consistent glueing data.

Since ordinary commutative rings
A
give commutative bios
\mathscr{P}_{A}\in\text{CBio}_{0}
and since all our definitions reduce to their classical analogues for

\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}_{A}
,
A\in C\text{Ring}
, ordinary schemes embeds fully faithfully in generalized
schemes,

\text{Sch}\hookrightarrow\text{GSch},(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}})\mapsto(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}}})

Theorem 3
.

The category
GSch
has fiber products, for
f_{i}\in\text{GSch}(X_{i},Y)
:

X_{0}\prod\limits_{Y}X_{1}
X_{0}
X_{1}
Y
f_{0}
f_{1}

Proof.

Exactly as for ordinary schemes. Write
Y=\bigcup\limits_{i}\text{spec}(B_{i})
,

X_{\varepsilon}=\bigcup\limits_{i,j}\text{spec}(A_{j,i}^{\varepsilon})
,
\varepsilon=0,1

with
f_{\varepsilon}\left(\text{spec}(A_{j,i}^{\varepsilon})\right)\subseteq\text{spec}\;(B_{i})
, and glue:

(7.8)

X_{0}\prod\limits_{Y}X_{1}\equiv\coprod_{i,j_{0},j_{1}}\text{spec}\left(A^{0}_{j_{0},i}\underset{B_{i}}{\boxtimes}A_{j_{1},i}^{1}\right).

Note that one uses the push-out

\left(A^{0}_{j_{0},i}\coprod\limits_{B_{i}}A_{j_{1},i}^{1}\right)^{C}
in
\text{CBio}_{0}

∎

8
Pro-Schemes

We have the full-embeddings

(8.1)

\text{Aff}\equiv\left(\cbio_{0}\right)^{\text{op}}\subseteq\text{GSch}\subseteq\cbio_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top}

The category
\cbio_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top}=\mathcal{C}
is complete. Given

\left\{\mathcal{X}_{i}\right\}\in\mathcal{C}^{I}
we have the topological space

\lim\limits_{\underset{I}{\leftarrow}}\mathcal{X}_{i}
, its points
x=(x_{i})
are
consistent sequences of points
x_{i}\in\mathcal{X}_{i}
, and we have the structure
sheaf
\mathscr{O}=\lim\limits_{\underset{I}{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}
over it
(with local stalks:
\mathscr{O}
being the co-limit of
local homomorphisms). The category
Aff
is also complete, as
\cbio_{0}
is
co-complete

(8.2)

\lim_{\underset{I}{\leftarrow}}\text{spec}(A_{i})\equiv\text{spec}(\lim\limits_{\underset{I}{\rightarrow}}A_{i})

But the category
GSch
, the full sub-category of

\cbio_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top}
with objects the ones locally isomorphic to elements of

Aff
, is not complete: Given
\left\{\mathcal{X}_{i}\right\}\in\left(\text{GSch}\right)^{I}
, and given a point

(8.3)

x=(x_{i})\in\lim_{\underset{I}{\leftarrow}}\mathcal{X}_{i}\in\cbio_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top}

while for each
i\in I
we have an affine neighborhood
x_{i}\in\text{spec}A_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
there need not be an affine neighborhood of
x
in

\lim\limits_{\underset{I}{\leftarrow}}\mathcal{X}_{i}
. Such is the case of the real prime.
So we pass to the pro-category pro-GSchm. It has objects
(J,\mathcal{X}_{j})
where
J
is a
partially ordered set which is

(8.4)

\text{directed:}\quad\forall j_{1},j_{2}\in J,\;\;\exists j\in J,\;\;j\geq j_{1}\;\;\text{and}\;\;j\geq j_{2}

(8.5)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\text{co-finite:}\quad\forall j\in J,\;\;\#\left\{i\in J,i\leq j\right\}<\infty\\
\\
\mathcal{X}_{j}\in(\text{GSch})^{J}\;:\quad j_{1}\geq j_{0}\;\;\Longrightarrow\;\;\mathcal{X}_{j_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{j_{0}}\end{array}

The maps in pro-
GSch
are given by

(8.6)

\text{pro-GSch}\left((J,\mathcal{X}_{j}),(I,Y_{i})\right)=\lim\limits_{\underset{I}{\leftarrow}}\lim\limits_{\underset{J}{\rightarrow}}\text{GSch}\left(\mathcal{X}_{j},Y_{i}\right)

We have a functor

(8.7)

\begin{array}[H]{llll}\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\;\;:&\text{pro-GSch}&\longrightarrow&\cbio_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top}\\
\\
&(J,\mathcal{X}_{j})&\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}&\lim\limits_{\overset{\longleftarrow}{J}}\mathcal{X}_{j}\end{array}

The category
GSch
embeds fully faithfully in pro-
GSch
; the category
pro-
GSch
is closed under limits over directed co-finite posets.

Example 8.8
.

Take

(8.9)

J=\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}\;\text{square free}\right\}=\left\{p_{1}\cdot p_{2}\cdots p_{\ell},p_{i}\;\text{prime},i\not=j\Rightarrow p_{i}\not=p_{j}\right\}

with
n_{1}\geq n_{0}
iff
n_{0}
divides
n_{1}
. We have the “
compactified

\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})
”

(8.10)

\overline{\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})}:=\left\{\mathcal{X}_{n}=\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})\coprod\limits_{\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z}\left[\frac{1}{n}\right])}\text{spec}\left(\mathbb{Z}\left[\frac{1}{n}\right]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}\right)\right\}_{n\in J}

\mathcal{X}_{n}

looks like

When
n|m
, the map
\mathcal{X}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{n}
is the identity on points, and
for
\mathscr{U}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{n}
open:

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{n}}(\mathscr{U})\equiv\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{m}}(\mathscr{U})
; but there
are more open neighborhood to
\eta_{m}\in\mathcal{X}_{m}
than there are for
\eta_{n}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}
.

9
Valuations

Definition 9.1
.

We say
K\in\cbio_{0}
is a
field
if

K(1)\setminus\left\{0\right\}\equiv K^{*}:=\left\{a\in K(1),\exists a^{-1}\in K(1),a\circ a^{-1}=1\right\}.

A
\text{sub-Bio}_{0}

B\subseteq K
is than a
domain
:
\left\{0\right\}
is prime and the localization gives embedding

(9.2)

B\subseteq B_{(0)}\subseteq K

We define

(9.3)

\begin{array}[H]{ll}B^{+}(n)^{\perp}=\left\{p\in K^{-}(n),p\circ B^{+}(n)\subseteq B(1)\right\}\\
\\
B^{-}(n)^{\perp}=\left\{q\in K^{+}(n),B^{-}(n)\circ q\subseteq B(1)\right\}\end{array}

For fields
k\subseteq K\in\cbio_{0}^{t}
(with involution!) we define the valuation-sub-bios

(9.4)

\text{Val}(K/k):=\left\{\begin{array}[H]{l}B\in\cbio_{0}^{t},\;k\subseteq B\subseteq B_{(0)}\equiv K,\\
\\
B^{\pm}(n)^{\perp}=B^{\mp}(n)\\
\\
\forall x\in K^{\ast},\;x\in B(1)\;\;\text{or}\;\;x^{-1}\in B(1)\end{array}\right\}

We define the real-valued valuations

(9.5)

\text{Val}_{\mathbb{R}}\left(K/k\right):=\left\{\begin{array}[H]{l}|\;|=|\;|_{n,\pm}:K^{\pm}(n)\longrightarrow[0,\infty)\\
\\
|x_{1}\circ x_{2}|_{1}=|x_{1}|_{1}\cdot|x_{2}|_{1},\quad|x|_{1}=0\Leftrightarrow x=0.\\
\\
\big|p\circ(p_{i})\big|\leq|p|\cdot\max|p_{i}|;\quad|(q_{i})\circ q|\leq|q|\cdot\max|q_{i}|;\\
\\
|p\circ(q_{i})|\leq|p|\cdot\max|q_{i}|;\quad|(p_{i})\circ q|\leq|q|\cdot\max|p_{i}|;\\
\\
|p|=|p^{t}|;\quad\forall\lambda\in k,|\lambda|\leq 1;\\
\\
|p|_{n,-}=\sup\left\{|p\circ q|_{1},|q|_{n+}\leq 1\right\}=\inf\left\{|d^{-1}|_{1},|d\circ p|_{n-}\leq 1\right\}\\
\\
|q|_{n,+}=\sup\left\{|p\circ q|_{1},|p|_{n-}\leq 1\right\}=\inf\left\{|d^{-1}|_{1},|q\circ d|_{n+}\leq 1\right\}\end{array}\right\}_{\text{\Huge$/\approx$}}

where
\approx
is the equivalence relation given by

||\approx||^{\sigma}\quad,\quad\sigma>0.

We have a map

(9.6)

\begin{array}[H]{rl}\text{Val}_{\mathbb{R}}\left(K/k\right)&\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.92 0 L 38.82 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{5.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.87 C 3.67 3.87 6.64 3.01 6.64 1.94 C 6.64 0.87 3.67 0 0 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{28.25276pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;\text{Val}\left(K/k\right)\\
\\
|\;|&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}B_{||}\equiv\left\{p\in K,|p|\leq 1\right\}\end{array}

and this map is a bijection if for all
B\in\text{Val}\left(K/k\right)
the set

K^{\ast}/B^{\ast}
, which is partially-ordered by

(9.7)

x\leq y\Longleftrightarrow x\circ y^{-1}\in B(1)

is class one and there is an order preserving embedding
|\;|_{1}:K^{\ast}/B^{\ast}\hookrightarrow(0,\infty)
.

Theorem 4

(Ostrowski Theorem)
.

We have

(9.8)

\text{Val}\left(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{F}\right)=\text{Val}_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{F}\right)=\left\{\mathbb{Q};\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}=\mathbb{Q}\cap\mathbb{Z}_{p},p\;\text{prime};\mathbb{Q}\cap\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}\right\}

For a number field
K
we have

(9.9)

\begin{array}[H]{ll}\text{Val}\left(K/\mathbb{F}\right)&=\text{Val}_{\mathbb{R}}\left(K/\mathbb{F}\right)\\
\\
&=\par\left\{\begin{array}[H]{l}K;\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathfrak{p}}=K\cap\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K,\mathfrak{p}},\mathfrak{p}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{K}\;\text{prime};\\
K\cap\sigma^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{C}}),\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},\sigma\sim\overline{\sigma}\end{array}\right\}\end{array}

Proof.

For a proof see
[
Har17
]

∎

Remark 9.10
.

The requirement that
B
is preserved by the involution of
K
,
|p^{t}|=|p|
, is
crutial: without it we have also the
\ell_{p}
-valuations
\mathbb{Q}\cap\left(\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{p}},\mathscr{P}_{\ell_{q}}\right)
, cf. §
3

10
Props

Definition 10.1
.

A
prop

\mathscr{P}
is a strict-symmetric-monoidal category which is generated
by one object
x
.

Thus we have sets
\mathscr{P}_{n,m}=\mathscr{P}\left(x^{\oplus m},x^{\oplus n}\right)
with an
action of
S_{n}\times S_{m}^{\text{op}}
and composition

(10.2)

\begin{array}[H]{rl}\mathscr{P}_{n,m}\times\mathscr{P}_{m,\ell}&\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathscr{P}_{n,\ell}\\
\\
f,g&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}f\circ g\end{array}

which is
S_{m}
-invariant,
S_{n}\times S_{\ell}^{\text{op}}
-covariant, and associative

(10.3)

(f\circ g)\circ h=f\circ(g\circ h)

and unital

(10.4)

1_{n}\circ f=f=f\circ 1_{m}\;,\;f\in\mathscr{P}_{n,m}\;,\;1_{n}\in\mathscr{P}_{n,n}

We also assume
\mathscr{P}_{0,m}=\left\{0\right\}
,
\mathscr{P}_{n,0}=\left\{0\right\}
, and

0
is an initial and final object of
\mathscr{P}
. The monoidal structure is given by

(10.5)

\begin{array}[H]{rl}\mathscr{P}_{n_{1},m_{1}}\times\mathscr{P}_{n_{2},m_{2}}&\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\mathscr{P}_{n_{1}+n_{2},m_{1}+m_{2}}\\
\\
f_{1},f_{2}\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}f_{1}\oplus f_{2}\end{array}

It is
S_{n_{1}}\times S_{n_{2}}\subseteq S_{n_{1}+n_{2}}
and
S_{m_{1}}\times S_{m_{2}}\subseteq S_{m_{1}+m_{2}}
covariant, functorial

(10.6)

\begin{array}[H]{c}(f_{1}\oplus f_{2})\circ(g_{1}\oplus g_{2})=(f_{1}\circ g_{1})\oplus(f_{2}\circ g_{2})\\
\\
1_{n+m}=1_{n}\oplus 1_{m}\end{array}

strict monoidal

(10.7)

\begin{array}[H]{c}(f_{1}\oplus f_{2})\oplus f_{3}=f_{1}\oplus(f_{2}\oplus f_{3})\\
f\oplus\text{id}_{0}=f=\text{id}_{0}\oplus f\end{array}

and symmetric

(10.8)

f_{2}\oplus f_{1}=\tau_{n_{1},n_{2}}\circ(f_{1}\oplus f_{2})\circ\tau_{m_{2},m_{1}}

with
\tau_{n,m}\in S_{n+m}
given by

(10.9)

\tau(i)=\left\{\begin{array}[H]{lc}n+i&i\leq m\\
\\
i-m&i>m\end{array}\right.

pictorially,

(10.10)

For props
\mathscr{P}
and
\mathscr{Q}
we put

(10.11)

\prop(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q})=\left\{\begin{array}[H]{l}f_{n,m}\in\text{Set}(\mathscr{P}_{n,m},\mathscr{Q}_{n,m}),f(\sigma\cdot p\cdot\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma\cdot f(p)\cdot\sigma,f(0)=0,\\
\\
f(p_{1}\circ p_{2})=f(p_{1})\circ f(p_{2}),f(1)=1,f(p_{1}\oplus p_{2})=f(p_{1})\oplus f(p_{2})\end{array}\right\}

so that we have a category
\prop
.

Remark 10.12
.

(cf.
1.14
-
1.16
). Given a strict symmetric monoidal category

\mathcal{E}
and object
x\in\mathcal{E}^{0}
we have the prop
\text{End}_{\mathcal{E}}(x)
with

(10.13)

\text{End}_{\mathcal{E}}(x)_{n,m}:=\mathcal{E}(x^{\oplus m},x^{\oplus n}).

Replacing everywhere Set by
\mathcal{E}
we obtain the category of props in

\mathcal{E}:\prop(\mathcal{E})
.

The category
\prop
has an involution
\mathscr{P}\mapsto\mathscr{P}^{\text{op}}
with

(10.14)

\mathscr{P}^{\text{op}}_{n,m}:=\mathscr{P}_{m,n}

We have therefore the props with an ivnolution
p\mapsto p^{t}
,

\mathscr{P}_{n,m}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{P}_{m,n}
and the category
\prop^{t}
(with maps
preserving the involutions).

Definition 10.15
.

A prop
\mathscr{P}
will be called
central
if the monoid
\mathscr{P}_{1,1}
is
commutative and
central
:

(10.16)

a\cdot p:=(\overset{n}{\oplus}\,a)\circ p\equiv p\circ(\overset{m}{\oplus}\,a)\quad\text{for $a\in\mathscr{P}_{1,1}$,
$p\in\mathscr{P}_{n,m}$}.

A prop
\mathscr{P}
will be called
commutative
if it is central and we have
for
b\in\mathscr{P}_{1,k}
,
p\in\mathscr{P}_{n,m}
,
d\in\mathscr{P}_{k,1}

(10.17)

(b\circ d)\cdot p\equiv(\overset{n}{\oplus}\,b)\circ\sigma_{n,k}\cdot(\overset{k}{\oplus}\,p)\circ\sigma_{k,m}\circ(\overset{m}{\oplus}\,d)

A prop
\mathscr{P}
will be called
totally-commuative
if we have for

p\in\mathscr{P}_{p_{0},p_{1}}
,
q\in\mathscr{P}_{q_{0},q_{1}}
,

(10.18)

p\otimes q:=(\overset{q_{0}}{\oplus}\,p)\circ\sigma_{q_{0},p_{1}}\circ(\overset{p_{1}}{\oplus}\,q)\equiv\sigma_{q_{0},p_{0}}(\overset{p_{0}}{\oplus}\,q)\sigma_{p_{0},q_{1}}(\overset{q_{1}}{\oplus}\,p)\sigma_{q_{1},p_{1}}

(here
\sigma_{n,m}\in S_{n\cdot m}
is the permutation (
4
)).

We let

(10.19)

with the frogetfull functor
U

(10.21)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\left(U\mathscr{P}\right)^{-}(n)=\mathscr{P}_{1,n}\quad,\quad(U\mathscr{P})^{+}(n)=\mathscr{P}_{n,1}\\
\\
p\circ(p_{i}):=p\circ{\oplus}p_{i},(q_{i})\circ q:=({\oplus}q_{i})\circ q\quad\text{etc.}\end{array}

The left adjoints
\mathcal{F}\mathscr{P}
give the free prop generated by the bio
\mathscr{P}
. Note
that when
\mathscr{P}
is (totally) commutative, the elements of
\left(\mathcal{F}_{C}\mathscr{P}\right)_{n,m}
, (resp.
\mathcal{F}_{T}\mathscr{P}
), can be written, non-uniquely, as

(10.22)

\begin{array}[H]{c}\text{``}\;\;\left(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\right)\circ\sigma\circ\left(\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{j=1}^{m}q_{j}\right)\;\;\text{''}\\
\\
p_{i}\in\mathscr{P}^{-}(k_{i}),\;q_{j}\in\mathscr{P}^{+}(\ell_{j}),\;\sigma\in S_{L},\;L=k_{1}+\cdots+k_{n}=\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{m}.\end{array}

Moreover, we have

(10.23)

\Big(\overset{n}{\oplus}p_{i}\Big)\circ\sigma\circ\Big(\overset{m}{\oplus}q_{j}\Big)\simeq\Big(\overset{n^{\prime}}{\hskip-2.84526pt\oplus}p^{\prime}_{i}\Big)\circ\sigma^{\prime}\circ\Big(\overset{m^{\prime}}{\hskip-2.84526pt\oplus}q_{j}^{\prime}\Big)

iff we have
\big(\overset{n}{\oplus}\varphi(p_{i})\big)\circ\sigma\circ\big(\overset{m}{\oplus}\varphi(q_{j})\big)=\big(\overset{n^{\prime}}{\hskip-2.84526pt\oplus}\varphi(p^{\prime}_{i})\big)\circ\sigma^{\prime}\circ\big(\overset{m^{\prime}}{\hskip-2.84526pt\oplus}\varphi(q_{j}^{\prime})\big)

for all
\varphi\in\bio(\mathscr{P},U\mathscr{Q})
and all
\mathscr{Q}\in\cprop
(resp.
\ctprop
).

For a rig

R
we have the prop
\mathscr{P}_{R}
with
(\mathscr{P}_{R})_{n,m}
the
n
by
m
matrices with
entries in
R
, composition is matrix multiplication, and
\oplus
is block-direct sum of
matrices. When
R
has an involution
\mathscr{P}_{R}
has an involution; for
A=(a_{ij})
in

(\mathscr{P}_{R})_{n,m}
we have
A^{t}=(b_{ji})
,
b_{ji}=a_{ij}^{t}
. When
R
is
commutative
\mathscr{P}_{R}
is totally-commutative and the diagram

(10.24)

For a
\prop

\mathscr{P}
we have the groups of invertible elements

(10.25)

\text{GL}_{n}(\mathscr{P}):=\left\{a\in\mathscr{P}_{n,n},\exists a^{-1}\in\mathscr{P}_{n,n},a^{-1}\circ a=a\circ a^{-1}=1_{n}\right\}

and homomorphisms

(10.26)

\begin{array}[H]{cl}\text{GL}_{n}(\mathscr{P})\times\text{GL}_{m}(\mathscr{P})&\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\text{GL}_{n+m}(\mathscr{P})\\
\\
a\quad,\quad b&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}a\oplus b\end{array}

(10.27)  E.g.

\begin{array}[h]{l}\\
\text{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{F})\equiv S_{n}\subseteq\text{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{F}[\pm 1])\equiv(\pm 1)^{n}\rtimes S_{n}\subseteq\text{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}})\equiv O(n)\subseteq\text{GL}(\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{C}})\equiv U(n)\end{array}

11
\prop
-Schemes

For a commutative prop
\mathscr{P}\in\cprop
we have the compact sober topological space

\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})\equiv\text{spec}(U\mathscr{P})
. Since
\mathscr{P}_{1,1}
acts centrally on the sets

\mathscr{P}_{n,m}
there is no problem with localizations with respect to multiplicative sets

S\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{1,1}
and we get cannonical map

(11.1)

\phi_{S}:\mathscr{P}\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}S^{-1}\mathscr{P}

In particular we have the localizations

(11.2)

\begin{array}[H]{l}\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}:=S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\mathscr{P}\quad,\quad S_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathscr{P}_{1,1}\setminus\mathfrak{p}\quad,\quad\mathfrak{p}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{1,1}\;\;\text{prime}\\
\\
\mathscr{P}_{f}:=S_{f}^{-1}\mathscr{P}\quad,\quad S_{f}=\left\{1,f,f^{2},\cdots f^{n}\cdots\right\}\quad,\quad f\in\mathscr{P}_{1,1}.\end{array}

Moreover, commutativity imply that there is a sheaf of commutative props
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{P}}

over
\text{spec}(\mathscr{P})
with stalks

(11.3)

\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{P},\mathfrak{p}}=\lim\limits_{\mathscr{U}\ni\mathfrak{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathscr{U})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{P}_{\mathfrak{p}}

and with global sections

(11.4)

\mathscr{P}_{f}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{P}}\big(D(f)\big).

We get a functor

(11.5)

\text{spec}:\big(\cprop)^{\text{op}}\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\cprop_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top}

with
\cprop_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top}
the category with objects
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
,

\mathcal{X}\in\text{Top}
,
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
a sheaf of commutative props with local stalks, and maps of
prop-spaces inducing local maps on stalks. We get the category of
Prop-Schemes

\mathscr{P}
Sch
:

(11.6)

(\cprop)^{\text{op}}\subseteq\text{$\mathscr{P}$Sch}\subseteq\cprop_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top},

it is the full subcategory of
\cprop_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top}
consisting of
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
such that for some open cover
\mathcal{X}=\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathscr{U}_{i}

(11.7)

\big(\mathscr{U}_{i},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\big|_{\mathscr{U}_{i}}\big)\cong\text{spec}\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathscr{U}_{i})\big)

The adjunction (
10.20
) prolongs to an adjunction, over
Top
, under Sch,

(11.8)

Example 11.10
.

Using the notations of (
8.11
), we have the “
compactified

\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})
” with the same underlying topological spaces as in
(
8.10
), (
8.11
), but with the sheaf of props
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}}
with

(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{n,m}(\mathscr{U})
the
n
by
m

\mathbb{Q}
-valued matrices
a=(a_{ij})
,

a_{ij}\in\mathbb{Q}
such that

(11.11)

a\circ\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{m}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\quad,\quad\text{all $p\in\mathscr{U}$},

and moreover,

(11.12)

a\circ\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\quad\text{if the real prime
$\eta\in\mathscr{U}$}

(with
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}^{n}=\left\{(x_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\sum|x_{i}|^{2}\leq 1\right\}
the unit

\ell_{2}
-balls). The global sections

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\overline{\text{spec}\mathbb{Z}})_{n,m}
are the
n
by
m

\mathbb{Z}
valued
matrices satisfying (
11.12
), which are the matrices having in each column
and in each raw at most one non-zero term with entry
1
or
-1
:

(11.13)

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\overline{\text{spec}\mathbb{Z}})\equiv\mathbb{F}[\pm 1]

12
The Witt ring and Frobenius correspondences

For a prop
\mathscr{P}\in\cprop
we have the group
\text{GL}_{n}(\mathscr{P})
of invertible elements of
the monoid
\mathscr{P}_{n,n}
and it acts on
\mathscr{P}_{n,n}
by conjugation; we let
[p]
denote
the conjugacy class of
p\in\mathscr{P}_{n,n}
and we let
[\mathscr{P}_{n,n}]
denote the collection
of these conjugacy classes. We have an embedding

(12.1)

\left[\mathscr{P}_{n,n}\right]\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}\left[\mathscr{P}_{n+1,n+1}\right]\;\;,[p]\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}[p\oplus 0],

and we take the direct limit:

(12.2)

[\mathscr{P}]:=\lim\limits_{\overset{\rightarrow}{n}}\left[\mathscr{P}_{n,n}\right]

We have a commutative monoid structure on
[\mathscr{P}]
via

(12.3)

[p_{1}]+[p_{2}]:=\left[p_{1}\oplus p_{2}\right]

and applying
K
(Grothendieck localization of addition) we get an abelian group, the Witt group:

(12.4)

\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P}):=K\left([\mathscr{P}]\right).

This group has the Frobenius endomorphisms given by

(12.5)

F_{n}:\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P})\rightarrow\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P})\;\;,\;\;F_{n}[p]=[p^{n}]=\left[\underbrace{p\circ p\circ\cdots\circ p}_{n}\right]

We thus have a functor

(12.6)

\mathcal{W}:\prop\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}(Ab)^{\mathbb{N}}

When the prop
\mathscr{P}
is
totally
commutative, we have multiplication on
[\mathscr{P}]
via, cf.
(
10.18
),

(12.7)

[p_{1}]\cdot[p_{2}]=[p_{1}\otimes p_{2}]

making
[\mathscr{P}]
into a commutative rig, and so
\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P})
is a commutative ring
and the
F_{n}
’s are ring endomorphisms:

(12.8)

\mathcal{W}:\ctprop\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}(\text{CRing})^{\mathbb{N}}

We can summarize our constructions in the following diagram:

(12.9)

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

Here
\left(\text{CRing}\right)^{\mathbb{N}^{+}}
is the category of commutative rings with an action
of the multiplicative monoid
\mathbb{N}^{+}
of positive natural numbers (the free commutative
monoid on the set of primes), and
\text{CRing}_{\text{loc}}/\text{Top}
is the usual category of
locally ringed spaces.

Given a prop-scheme
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
we can apply
\mathcal{W}
to the props

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathscr{U})
to obtain a sheaf of abelian groups

\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
over

\mathcal{X}
. When all the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathscr{U})
are totally commutative

\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
is a sheaf of commutative rings with Frobenius
endomorphisms.

Given an ordinary commutative ring
R\in\text{CRing}
, we have the
associated totally-commutative prop
\mathscr{P}_{R}\in\ctprop
and

\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P}_{R})
is the ring of rational Witt vectors, cf.
[
Alm74
]
,

(12.10)

\begin{array}[H]{rc}\displaystyle\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P}_{R})&\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-2.59998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.92 0 L 38.82 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{5.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.87 C 3.67 3.87 6.64 3.01 6.64 1.94 C 6.64 0.87 3.67 0 0 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{28.25276pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;1+x\cdot R\left[[x]\right]\\
\\
\displaystyle\left[p_{1}\right]-\left[p_{2}\right]&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt}\frac{\det(1-x\cdot p_{1})}{\det(1-x\cdot p_{2})}.\end{array}

For a domain
R
, with fraction field
K
, let
\overline{K}
be an algebraic
closure of
K
,
G_{K}=\text{Gal}\left(\overline{K}/K\right)
,

\overline{R}
the integral closure of
R
in
\overline{K}
, than

\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P}_{R})
is the free abelian group on monic irreducible
polynomials
f(x)\not=x
, via the correspondence

(12.11)

f(x)=x^{n}+a_{1}x^{n-1}+\cdots+a_{n}\longleftrightarrow\tilde{f}(x)=x^{\deg f}\cdot f\left(\frac{1}{x}\right)=1+a_{1}x+\cdots+a_{n}x^{n}

Alternatively,
\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P}_{R})
is the free abelian group on
G_{K}

orbits in
\overline{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
:

(12.12)

\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P}_{R})=\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{[\alpha]\in\left(\overline{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\right)/G_{K}}\mathbb{Z}[\alpha]

with
[\alpha]=\{\alpha=\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n}\}
the
G_{K}
-conjugates of
\alpha
.
The multiplication in
\mathcal{W}(\mathscr{P}_{R})
is given by multiplying orbits:

(12.13)

[\alpha]\cdot[\beta]=\left\{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n}\right\}\cdot\left\{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{m}\right\}=\left\{\alpha_{i}\cdot\beta_{j}\right\}

13
The ring
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{W}(\overline{\text{spec}\mathbb{Z}})

For the compactified
\overline{\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})}=\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})\cup\left\{\eta\right\}
, the condition (
11.12
) at the real prime implies that all the
eigenvalues of
a
are in the unit disc, hence the global sections

\mathcal{W}(\overline{\text{spec}(\mathbb{Z})})
is the free abelian group on

G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-orbits of algebraic integers
[\alpha]=\left\{\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n}\right\}
with
|\alpha_{i}|\leq 1
, hence they are roots of unity, and so

(13.1)

\mathcal{W}(\overline{\text{spec}\mathbb{Z}})\equiv\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{Z}\cdot\phi_{n}=\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{Z}[\bbmu_{n}^{\ast}]

with
\phi_{n}
the cyclotomic polynomial with roots the primitive
n
-th roots of unity

\bbmu_{n}^{*}\cdot
. The multiplication is given by

(13.2)

\phi_{n}\cdot\phi_{m}=\phi_{n\cdot m}\quad\text{for}\quad(n,m)=1,

(13.3)

\begin{array}[H]{ll}\phi_{p^{n}}\cdot\phi_{p^{m}}=\left\{\begin{array}[H]{lc}(1-p^{-1})p^{m}\cdot\phi_{p^{n}}&n>m\geq 1\\
\\
(1-p^{-1})p^{n}\cdot\left[\phi_{p^{n}}+\phi_{p^{n-1}}+\cdots+\phi_{p}+\phi_{1}\right]-p^{n-1}\cdot\phi_{p^{n}}&n=m\geq 1\end{array}\right.\end{array}

The algebra
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{W}(\overline{spec\mathbb{Z}})
is the union of its finite subalgebras

(13.4)

\mathcal{W}=\bigcup_{N\geq 1}\mathcal{W}_{N}\quad,\quad\mathcal{W}_{N}=\mathop{\oplus}_{d\mid N}\mathbb{Z}\phi_{d}

and is the tensor product over all primes (with respect to
\phi_{1}=1
)

(13.5)

\mathcal{W}=\otimes_{p}\mathcal{W}_{p^{\infty}}\quad,\quad\mathcal{W}_{p^{\infty}}=\oplus_{\ell\geq 0}\mathbb{Z}\phi_{p^{\ell}},

with
\mathcal{W}_{p^{n}}/\mathcal{W}_{p^{n-1}}
being an integral quadratic extension (
13.3
).

The Frobenius ring endomorphisms are completely multiplicative

(13.6)

F_{m_{1}}\circ F_{m_{2}}=F_{m_{1}\cdot m_{2}}

and are given for
p
prime by

(13.7)

F_{p}\phi_{n}=\left\{\begin{array}[h]{ll}\phi_{n}&p\not\mid n\\
\\
p\cdot\phi_{n/p}&p^{2}\mid n\\
\\
(p-1)\cdot\phi_{n/p}&p\mid n\;,\;p^{2}\not\mid n\end{array}\right.

or generally by

(13.8)

F_{m}\phi_{n}=(m,n)\cdot\Big(\prod\limits_{\begin{array}[h]{c}p\mid(m,n)\\
p\not\mid n/(m,n)\end{array}}(1-p^{-1})\Big)\cdot\phi_{n/(m,n)}\;\;,\;\;(m,n)=\gcd(m,n)

We have a cannonical ring homomorphism

(13.9)

\begin{array}[h]{r}t_{1}=\text{tr}:\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}{}\mathbb{Z}\\
\text{$[a]$}\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}{}\text{tr}\;a\end{array}

and

(13.10)

\text{tr}(\phi_{n})=\sum_{\xi\in\bbmu_{n}^{\ast}}\xi=\mu(n)

is the Möbius function. We obtain the homomorphisms

(13.11)

\begin{array}[h]{ll}t_{m}=\text{tr}\circ F_{m}:&\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathbb{Z}\\
\text{$[a]$}\xmapsto{\hskip 8.19447pt}\text{tr}(a^{m})\end{array}

and

(13.12)

t_{m}(\phi_{n})=\text{tr}(F_{m}\phi_{n})=\sum_{\xi\in\bbmu_{n}^{\ast}}\xi^{m}=C_{n}^{m}

with the Ramanujan sums

(13.13)

C_{n}^{m}=\mu\left(\frac{n}{(n,m)}\right)\cdot\frac{\varphi(n)}{\varphi(\frac{n}{(n,m)})}\;\;,\;\;n\geq 1\;\;,\;\;m\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\right)/\left(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\ast}

Note that

(13.14)

m\equiv 0\pmod{n}\Longrightarrow F_{m}\phi_{n}=\varphi(n)\cdot\phi_{1}

and we get by continuity an action of the multiplicative monoid of “super-natural-number”

(13.15)

\hat{\mathbb{Z}}/\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\ast}=\prod_{p\text{ prime}}p^{\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}

by ring endomorphisms of
\mathcal{W}
. In particular, we get the homomorphism

(13.16)

F_{0}=\lim_{m\to 0\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}}F_{m}\;:\;\mathcal{W}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}.

We have
F_{0}\circ F_{m}=F_{0}
for all
m
, and

(13.17)

F_{0}\phi_{n}=\#\bbmu_{n}^{\ast}=\varphi(n)

is Euler’s function.

The group of roots of unity
\bbmu_{\infty}
give rise to the group-ring
\mathbb{Z}\cdot\bbmu_{\infty}
, which maps homomorphically onto the ring of cyclotomic integers
\mathbb{Z}[\bbmu_{\infty}]
, and also maps onto
\mathbb{Z}
via the augmutation. Taking
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}=\text{Aut}(\bbmu_{\infty})
invariants we get

(13.18)

\displaystyle\hbox to184.31pt{\vbox to70.85pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.58443pt\lower 10.2942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
