Remark 13.19
.

We have

\text{CRing}(\mathcal{W},\mathbb{C})\equiv\text{CRing}(\mathcal{W},\mathbb{Z})\equiv\hat{\mathbb{Z}}/\hat{Z}^{*}

Indeed, from (??) we obain

\text{CRing}(\mathcal{W},\mathbb{C})\equiv\prod_{p}\text{CRing}(\mathcal{W}_{p^{\infty}},\mathbb{C}).

From (??), we obtain for
\psi\in\text{CRing}(\mathcal{W}_{p^{\infty}},\mathbb{C})
the implications for
n\geq 1
,

\begin{array}[h]{lll}&&\displaystyle\psi(\phi_{p^{n}})\not=0\\
\\
\displaystyle\Longrightarrow&&\psi(\phi_{p^{m}})=p^{m}\left(1-p^{-1}\right)\text{ for $m=1,2,\ldots,n-1$}\\
\\
\displaystyle\Longrightarrow&&\psi(\phi_{p^{n}})=p^{n}\left(1-p^{-1}\right)\text{ or $\psi(\phi_{p^{n}})=-p^{n-1}$}\end{array}

From this it follows that
\psi=\text{tr}\circ F_{p^{n}}
,
n\geq 0
,

\text{tr}\circ F_{p^{n}}(\phi_{p^{m}})=\left\{\begin{array}[h]{ll}1&m=0\\
p^{m}(1-p^{-1})&1\leq m\leq n\\
-p^{n}&m=n+1\\
0&m>n+1\end{array}\right.

or that
\psi=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\text{tr}\circ F_{p^{n}}=F_{p^{\infty}}=F_{0}
. Thus

\text{CRing}(\mathcal{W}_{p^{\infty}},\mathbb{C})\equiv p^{\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}

∎

We have as well the additive projection

(13.20)

\int\;:\;\mathcal{W}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\quad,\quad\int\phi_{n}=\left\{\begin{array}[h]{ll}1&n=1\\
0&n>1\end{array}\right.

and we have

(13.21)

\int(\phi_{n_{1}}\cdot\phi_{n_{2}})=\left\{\begin{array}[h]{ll}\varphi(n)&n_{1}=n_{2}=n\\
\\
0&n_{1}\not=n_{2}\end{array}\right.

We can extend scalar to
\mathbb{C}
, and obtain the ring homomorphisms

(13.22)

t_{m}=\text{tr}\circ F_{m}:\mathcal{W}_{\mathbb{C}}=\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathcal{W}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}.

The projection
\int:\mathcal{W}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
can be expanded using the
t_{m}
’s as

(13.23)

\int f\equiv\lim_{M\to 0\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}}\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}t_{m}(f).

We can complete
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbb{C}}
with respect to the state
\int
and obtain the Hilbert space

(13.24)

\mathcal{H}=\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathbb{C}}\quad,\quad\langle f,g\rangle=\int(f\cdot\overline{g})

with orthogonal basis
\{\phi_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}\;\;,\;\;\|\phi_{n}\|^{2}=\varphi(n)
.

The complete-multiplicativity of the
F_{m}
, (
13.6
), suggests considering

(13.25)

\zeta(F,s):=\sum_{m\geq 1}\frac{1}{m^{s}}F_{m}\equiv\prod_{p}\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{s}}F_{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2s}}F_{p^{2}}+\cdots\right)\equiv\prod_{p}\frac{1}{(1-p^{-s}F_{p})}

The adoint of
F_{m}
, the “verschiebung”
F_{m}^{\ast}
is the
additive map given by
F_{m}^{\ast}\phi_{n}=\phi_{m\cdot n}
. The
multiplicativity
F^{\ast}_{m_{1}}\circ F_{m_{2}}^{\ast}=F_{m_{1}\cdot m_{2}}^{\ast}
suggest considering similarly

(13.26)

\zeta(F^{\ast},s)=\sum\limits_{m\geq 1}\frac{1}{m^{s}}F_{m}^{\ast}=\prod_{p}\frac{1}{(1-p^{-s}F_{p}^{\ast})}=\zeta(F,\overline{s})^{\ast}

In terms of these zeta elements we can interpret
Ramanujan’s sums

(13.27)

t_{m}\big(\zeta(F^{\ast},t)\phi_{1}\big)=\text{tr}\big(F_{m}\zeta(F^{\ast},t)\phi_{1}\big)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{C_{n}^{m}}{n^{t}}=\frac{1}{\zeta(t)}\cdot\left(\sum_{d\mid m}d^{1-t}\right)

(13.28)

\text{tr}\left(\zeta(F,s)\phi_{n}\right)=\sum_{m\geq 1}\frac{C_{n}^{m}}{m^{s}}=\zeta(s)\cdot\left(\sum_{d\mid n}\mu\left(\frac{n}{d}\right)d^{1-s}\right)

(13.29)

\text{tr}\left(\zeta(F,s)\zeta(F^{\ast},t)\phi_{1}\right)=\sum_{n,m\geq 1}\frac{C_{n}^{m}}{n^{t}m^{s}}=\frac{\zeta(s)}{\zeta(t)}\cdot\zeta(s+t-1)

with
\zeta(s)
the Riemann zeta function.

The probability measure
\frac{1}{M}\sum\limits_{m=1}^{M}\delta_{m}
on
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
, converges as
M\to 0\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
, to the Haar measure

dz
, and we get an isomorphism, the Fourier transform

(13.30)

\begin{array}[h]{rcl}\mathcal{H}=\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathbb{C}}&\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt\sim\hskip 16.38895pt}&L_{2}(\hat{\mathbb{Z}},dz)^{\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\ast}}\\
\\
f&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 16.38895pt}&\hat{f}(z):=\text{tr}(F_{z}f)\\
\\
F_{m}&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 16.38895pt}&\hat{F}_{m}\hat{f}(z):=\hat{f}(mz)\\
\\
F_{m}^{\ast}&\xmapsto{\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 16.38895pt}&\hat{F}_{m}^{\ast}\hat{f}(z):=m\cdot\hat{f}(z/m)\\
\\
\langle f,g\rangle&=&\hskip-25.60747pt\displaystyle\int\limits_{\hat{\mathbb{Z}}}\hat{f}(z)\cdot\overline{\hat{g}(z)}\,dz\par\end{array}

The ring
\mathcal{W}
has a structure of a special-
\lambda
-ring, with
\lambda
-operations.

(13.31)

\lambda^{n}:\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{W}\quad,\quad\lambda^{0}(x)=1\quad,\quad\lambda^{1}(x)=x\quad,\quad\lambda^{n}(x_{1}+x_{2})=\sum\limits_{n_{1}+n_{2}=n}\lambda^{n_{1}}(x_{1})\cdot\lambda^{n_{2}}(x_{2})

defined by

(13.32)

\begin{array}[h]{rll}\lambda_{t}(x)=\sum_{n\geq 0}(-1)^{n}\lambda^{n}(x)\cdot t^{n}:&\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}1+&\hskip-8.53581ptt\cdot\mathcal{W}[|t|]_{\text{rat}}\\
&\rotatebox{270.0}{\huge$\supseteq$}&\rotatebox{270.0}{\huge$\supseteq$}\\
\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{N}\cdot\phi_{n}\equiv&\mathcal{W}^{+}\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}1+&t\cdot\mathcal{W}[t]\end{array}

(13.33)

\lambda_{t}(\phi_{n})=\prod\limits_{\xi\in\bbmu_{n}^{*}}(1-[\xi]t)=\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\varphi(n)}\lambda^{m}(\phi_{n})\cdot(-t)^{m}

(13.34) E.g.

\begin{array}[h]{l}\lambda_{t}(\phi_{1})=1-t\\
\\
\lambda_{t}(\phi_{2})=1-t\cdot\phi_{2}\\
\\
\lambda_{t}(\phi_{3})=1-t\phi_{3}+t^{2}\\
\\
\lambda_{t}(\phi_{4})=1-t\phi_{4}+t^{2}\\
\\
\lambda_{t}(\phi_{5})=1-t\phi_{5}+t^{2}(\phi_{5}+2\phi_{1})-t^{3}\phi_{5}+t^{4}\\
\\
\lambda_{t}(\phi_{6})=1-t\phi_{6}+t^{2}\\
\\
\lambda_{t}(\phi_{7})=1-t\phi_{7}+t^{2}(2\cdot\phi_{7}+3\phi_{1})-t^{3}(3\phi_{7}+2\phi_{1})+t^{4}(2\phi_{7}+3\phi_{1})-t^{5}\phi_{7}+t^{6}\\
\\
\lambda_{t}(\phi_{8})=1-t\phi_{8}+t^{2}(\phi_{4}+2\phi_{2}+2\phi_{1})-t^{3}\phi_{8}+t^{4}\\
\\
\lambda_{t}(\phi_{9})=1-t\phi_{9}+t^{2}(\phi_{9}+3\phi_{3}+3\phi_{1})-t^{3}(3\phi_{9}+\phi_{3})+t^{4}(\phi_{9}+3\phi_{3}+3\phi_{1})-t^{5}\phi_{9}+t^{6}\\
\\
\lambda_{t}(\phi_{10})=1-t\phi_{10}+t^{2}(\phi_{5}+2\phi_{1})-t^{3}\phi_{10}+t^{4}\end{array}

We have,

(13.35)

\lambda_{t}(\phi_{n})=t^{\varphi(n)}\cdot\lambda_{t^{-1}}(\phi_{n})\qquad\text{for $n>2$}

(13.36)

\text{tr}\big(\lambda_{t}(\phi_{n})\big)=\prod\limits_{\xi\in\bbmu_{n}^{*}}(1-\xi\cdot t)=\tilde{\phi}_{n}(t)=\phi_{n}(t)

(13.37)

F_{0}\big(\lambda_{t}(\phi_{n})\big)=(1-t)^{\varphi(n)}

(13.38)

\lambda^{n}(k\cdot\phi_{1})=\begin{pmatrix}k\\
n\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{satisfies}\quad(-1)^{n}\lambda^{n}(-k\cdot\phi_{1})=\begin{pmatrix}k+n-1\\
n\end{pmatrix}=\lambda^{n}(k+n-1)

We denote the Grothendieck involution by

(13.39)

D(t)=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
1&-1\end{bmatrix}(t)=\frac{t}{t-1}=\frac{1}{1-t^{-1}}

so that
DD(t)=t
,
D(0)=0
,
D(1)=\infty
,
D(2)=2
, and in the notations of §??,
1/p+1/q=1\Leftrightarrow q=D(p)
. We obtain Grothendieck’s gamma-operations

(13.40)

\begin{array}[h]{l}\gamma_{t}(x)=\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}(-1)^{n}\gamma^{n}(x)t^{n}:\;\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}1+t\mathcal{W}[|t|]_{\text{rat}}\\
\\
\gamma_{t}^{0}(x)=1,\quad-\gamma^{1}(x)=x,\quad\gamma^{n}(x_{1}+x_{2})=\sum\limits_{n_{1}+n_{2}=n}\gamma^{n_{1}}(x_{1})\cdot\gamma^{n_{2}}(x_{2})\end{array}

by setting

(13.41)

\gamma_{t}(x)=\lambda_{D(t)}(x)\Leftrightarrow(-1)^{n}\gamma^{n}(x)=\lambda^{n}(x+n-1)\quad,\text{cf. (~{?}?)}

We have

(13.42)

\mathcal{W}=\mathbb{Z}\cdot\phi_{1}\oplus I\quad,\quad I=\ker F_{0}=\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{n>1}\mathbb{Z}(\phi_{n}-\varphi(n)\cdot\phi_{1})

The gamma-filteration is given by
I_{0}=\mathcal{W}
,
I_{1}=I
, and

(13.43)

I_{n}\equiv\text{additive subgroup of $\mathcal{W}$ generated by $\gamma^{n_{1}}(a_{1})\cdots\gamma^{n_{\ell}}(a_{\ell}),a_{i}\in I,\quad\sum n_{i}\geq n$}

It is a
\lambda
-filtration, and on the associated graded ring

(13.44)

\text{gr}_{\gamma}\mathcal{W}\equiv\mathop{\oplus}_{n\geq 0}I_{n}/I_{n+1}

we have

(13.45)

\ \hskip 68.2866ptF_{m}\equiv\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{n\geq 0}m^{n}\qquad\text{c.f. \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{AT68}{}{}]} prop. 5.3.}

and also

(13.46)

\ \hskip 39.83385pt(-1)^{m+1}\lambda^{m}\equiv\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{n\geq 1}m^{n-1}\qquad\text{c.f. \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{AT68}{}{}]} prop. 5.5.}

Note that

(13.47)

\ \ \hskip 62.59605pt\gamma_{t}:I^{+}\equiv\mathop{\oplus}\limits_{n>1}\mathbb{N}\cdot(\phi_{n}-\varphi(n)\phi_{n})\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}1+t\mathcal{W}[t]

and

(13.48)

\gamma_{t}(\phi_{n}-\varphi(n)\phi_{1})=(1-t)^{\varphi(n)}\cdot\prod\limits_{\xi\in\bbmu_{n}^{*}}\big(1-[\xi]\cdot\frac{t}{t-1}\big)=\prod\limits_{\xi\in\bbmu_{n}^{*}}\big(1-(1-[\xi])t\big)

i.e. passing from
\lambda^{m}(\phi_{n})
to
\gamma^{m}(\phi_{n}-\varphi(1)\phi_{1})
amounts to passing from the
m
-th elementary symmetric function of the roots of unity
\bbmu_{n}^{*}
, to that of the “cyclomic units”
1-[\xi]
,
\xi\in\bbmu_{n}^{*}
.

Remark 13.49
.

One can proceed as in
[
Den18
]
, inverting the
F_{n}
’s to get an action of the
multiplciative group of positive rational numbers
\mathbb{Q}^{+}=\oplus p^{\mathbb{Z}}
, the
free abelian group on the primes, and extend scalars along
\mathbb{Q}^{+}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{+}
; the infinitesimal generator of the
\mathbb{R}^{+}
action should give an
interesting operation when acting on appropriate cohomology groups.

Viewed from the perspective of the Fourier transform (??),
“inverting the
F_{n}
’s” amount to passing from
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}/\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}

to

\mathbb{Q}\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}\hat{\mathbb{Z}}/\hat{Z}^{*}=A_{\text{fin}}/\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}
; “extending scalars
along
\mathbb{Q}^{+}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{+}
” amount essentially to the passage to

\mathbb{A}_{\text{fin}}\oplus\mathbb{R}/_{\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\mathbb{Q}^{*}}=\mathbb{A}/_{\mathbb{Q}^{*}\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}}
.
This space and the action of

\mathbb{A}^{*}/_{\mathbb{Q}^{*}\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}}\equiv\mathbb{R}^{+}
on it, was first
suggested by Weil
[
Wei66
]
, and was farther studied in
[
Har91
]
,
[
Har01
]
, and by
Connes
[
Con99
]
. But note that for a curve
X
, over a
finite field
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, we do
not
have a proof of the
Riemann-hypothesis by simply using the action of
A^{*}_{K}/_{K^{*}}
on
A_{K}/_{K^{*}}
,
K=\mathbb{F}_{g}(X)
the function field of
X
; we realy
need the intersection theory on the surface
X\times X
.

*
